Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA World + 15 more Epidemic and emerging disease alerts in the Pacific as of 06 October 2026 Format Map Source SPC Posted 6 Oct 2026 Originally published 6 Oct 2026 Attachments Download Map (PDF | 590.42 KB) Highlights/updates since the last map was sent on PacNet on 29 September 2026: Dengue • Marshall Islands: The 10th Outbreak Situation Report on dengue in Majuro and Ebeye, dated 28 September 2026 and covering 15 August to 25 September, records 297 specimens tested (Majuro 263, Ebeye 34) and 112 confirmed positive (Majuro 97, Ebeye 15), with no deaths and seven cumulative hospitalisations. Four cases had onset in the past seven days (Majuro 3, Ebeye 1). The confirmed-case attack rate is 4.2 per 1,000 in Majuro and 1.5 per 1,000 in Ebeye, and the Majuro positivity rate is 36.8 percent. Only DENV-1 has been detected. The red alert for dengue (DENV-1) remains in effect. – Source: Republic of the Marshall Islands Ministry of Health and Human Services, Outbreak of Dengue Fever Cases in Majuro & Ebeye, 10th Outbreak Situation Report shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 01 October 2026. • Samoa: The Dengue Fever Outbreak Situation Report Issue No. 76 for EpiWeek 39 (21 to 27 September 2026) reports 35 new cases, a 169 percent increase on the previous week, including five laboratory-confirmed cases, against one the week before. Two new admissions and no deaths were reported. Since 1 January 2025, 20,308 clinically diagnosed cases, 5,546 laboratory-confirmed cases and nine dengue-related deaths have been recorded. DENV-1 accounts for 52 percent and DENV-2 for 48 percent of serotyped cases, and children under 15 years for 73 percent of cases. The outbreak will be declared over after 28 days without a new laboratory-confirmed case. The blue alert for dengue (DENV-1 & DENV-2) remains in effect. – Source: Ministry of Health Samoa, Dengue Fever Outbreak Situation Report Issue No. 76, shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 06 October 2026. • Vanuatu: Situation Report #14 on the dengue outbreak on Efate and Santo, dated 28 September 2026, reports 17 new laboratory-confirmed cases in EpiWeeks 38 and 39 (14 to 27 September), 10 from Efate and 7 from Santo, bringing the cumulative total to 169 confirmed cases. Weekly numbers remain above the outbreak threshold of four confirmed cases per week, although they have declined since the peak of EpiWeeks 33 to 35. In Santo, cases are now reported in Luganville, East Santo and Canal-Fanafo Area Councils. Sixteen dengue-associated hospitalisations have been reported, all discharged, with no severe dengue and no deaths. DENV-1 remains the only serotype detected. Tafea Provincial Health has also declared a dengue outbreak in Tafea Province, and its response team is investigating cases, tracing contacts and raising community awareness in the West Tanna Area Council. The red alert for dengue (DENV-1) remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Sources: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Dengue Outbreak on Efate and Santo Island, Situation Report #14, shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 1st October 2026, Official dengue press released for Tanna - Tafea Health Promotion official Facebook page and Tafea Provincial Health, Dengue response West Tanna accessed on 06 October 2026. • Wallis and Futuna: The Health Agency reported on 1st October 2026 that the dengue epidemic is continuing, with 11 new confirmed cases over weeks 37 to 39, four on Wallis and seven on Futuna, bringing the cumulative total for the territory to 95 confirmed or probable cases. The red alert for dengue remains in effect for Wallis and Futuna. – Source: Agence de Santé de Wallis et Futuna, epidemiological and health watch update for weeks 37 to 39 (1st October 2026) shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 06 October 2026. Measles • Australia: NSW Health issued a measles alert for the Campbelltown area on 03 October 2026 after a confirmed case in a returned traveller from South-East Asia. While infectious, the person visited several sites in the Campbelltown area and locations in Concord and Rooty Hill. As of 06 October 2026, 119 measles notifications have been reported nationally in Australia in 2026, already exceeding the annual totals for each year from 2020 to 2024 (25, 0, 7, 26 and 57 respectively), following 181 notifications in 2025, the highest annual count since 2019 (285). In 2026, New South Wales has reported the largest number of notifications (n=55), followed by Victoria (n=30), Queensland (n=18), South Australia (n=9), Western Australia (n=5) and the Australian Capital Territory (n=2). No cases have been notified in the Northern Territory or Tasmania. The red alert for measles remains in effect for Australia. – Sources: NSW Health, Measles alert for Campbelltown area and National Notifiable Disease Surveillance Syestem - Australian Centre fo Disease Control accessed on 5 October 2026. • Papua New Guinea: The National Department of Health weekly bulletin for EpiWeek 39 (21 to 27 September 2026) reports 17 laboratory-confirmed measles cases in 2026, up from 13 the previous week: National Capital District 6, East Sepik 4, Western Highlands 2, and one each in the Autonomous Region of Bougainville, Central, Eastern Highlands, Enga and New Ireland. The last onset date is 15 September 2026. Rubella cases rose from 21 to 25, and ten patients are positive for both measles and rubella. Year to date, 361 acute fever and rash cases have been reported and 373 samples submitted. The measles-rubella vaccination campaign targets all children under seven in districts with a detected case. The red alert for measles remains in effect for Papua New Guinea. – Source: Papua New Guinea National Department of Health, Public Health Events Weekly Bulletin, EpiWeek 39 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 02 October 2026. Pertussis • Vanuatu: Situation Report 05 on the pertussis outbreak in Penama Province, dated 28 September 2026, reports 21 new cases on Ambae Island in EpiWeeks 38 and 39 (respectively 1 and 20), bringing the cumulative total to 605 cases, of which 4 are laboratory confirmed. The outbreak peaked in EpiWeeks 36 and 37 (266 and 307 clinically diagnosed cases) and case numbers have since declined substantially, but continued detection shows that community transmission has not been interrupted. Two cases have been hospitalised since the start of the outbreak and no death is reported. North Ambae accounts for 54 percent of cases and South Ambae for 43 percent. The red alert for pertussis remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Source: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Pertussis Outbreak in Penama Province, Situation Report 5 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 01 October 2026. Respiratory diseases • New Caledonia: The Directorate of Health and Social Affairs (DASS) announced on 1st October 2026 that New Caledonia has entered an influenza epidemic phase. In week 39 (21 to 27 September), 115 new laboratory-confirmed cases were recorded, bringing the 2026 total to 238, 190 of them since week 33. A/H1N1pdm09 predominates and laboratory positivity reached 56 percent. Acute respiratory infections exceeded 15 percent of sentinel GP consultations in weeks 38 and 39. In week 39, 221 emergency department presentations were for acute respiratory infection (12 percent of all presentations, against 4 percent in week 37) and 54 patients were hospitalised, with a median age of 71 years. Since 27 September, bed occupancy in intensive care and continuous monitoring units has exceeded 75 percent. People aged 65 and over account for 36 percent of confirmed cases. The epidemic comes earlier than in 2024 and 2025. A red alert for influenza A is issued for New Caledonia. – Source: Direction des Affaires Sanitaires et Sociales de Nouvelle-Calédonie, Vigilance DASS bulletin 2026-#08, influenza epidemic, shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 02 October 2026. • New Zealand: For the week ending 27 September 2026, the all-cause SARI hospitalisation rate in the Auckland region has decreased into the low activity band, and the influenza-positive SARI rate decreased for the fourth week in a row and is now low. Influenza remained the most commonly detected virus among SARI patients, with test positivity stable at 32.9 percent, while positivity among sentinel GP influenza-like illness cases fell from 34.3 to 27.3 percent. A/H1 and A/H3 are detected at similar levels. Twenty-eight influenza-positive SARI patients have been admitted to intensive care in 2026, all since 27 July. Since 1st January, 5,296 influenza-positive specimens have been reported nationally (4,912 influenza A and 384 influenza B), and circulating viruses match the 2026 vaccine. Pacific peoples have had the highest cumulative rates of influenza-, RSV- and SARS-CoV-2-positive SARI hospitalisation in 2026. The red alert for influenza A is changed to a blue alert for New Zealand. – Sources: PHF Science, New Zealand Acute Respiratory Illness dashboard, accessed on 5 October 2026 and PHF Science, Virology Weekly Report, week 39, accessed on 5 October 2026. Rotavirus • Federated States of Micronesia: Situation Reports #18 and #19 on the diarrhoeal outbreak in Yap State, dated 25 September and 1st October 2026, report one new case on 25 September and nine between 28 September and 1st October, two of them from Ifalik Dispensary in the Outer Islands, bringing the cumulative total to 240. There have been 14 admissions since the outbreak began, 12 of them children, and no deaths. Rotavirus remains predominant, mostly as co-infections with E. coli, adenovirus, Salmonella, Giardia and, newly, Clostridioides difficile, and a laboratory-confirmed rotavirus reinfection was detected in a two-year-old. Children aged 0 to 11 years account for 66 percent of cases. Case counts have declined to low single digits since mid-September but remain above the alert threshold. The red alert for rotavirus is changed to a blue alert for the Federated States of Micronesia (Yap State). – Source: Yap State Diarrhoeal Outbreak Situation Reports #18 and #19 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 29 September and 02 October 2026. • Vanuatu: Situation Report No. 6 on the rotavirus outbreak in Tafea Province, dated 30 September 2026, reports 123 acute watery diarrhoea cases in EpiWeek 39 (21 to 27 September), bringing the cumulative total to 1,140 cases, three of them laboratory confirmed. Case numbers declined in EpiWeeks 38 and 39 but remain above the alert threshold of 34 cases per week. Cases have been reported on Aneityum Island for the first time, with 12 cases in EpiWeek 39. Children under five account for most cases. A total of 44 patients have been hospitalised since EpiWeek 33 and one death, in a one-year-old child, was reported in EpiWeek 35. Water testing detected E. coli contamination. The Ministry of Health has also declared a rotavirus outbreak in Sanma Province, on Santo, where more than 100 acute watery diarrhoea cases were reported between 24 August and 20 September and four confirmed cases in the latest week met the declaration criteria. The red alert for rotavirus remains in effect for Vanuatu. – Sources: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Rotavirus Outbreak in Tanna and Aneityum Island, Situation Report No. 6 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 01 October 2026 and Vanuatu Daily Post, Water diarrhoea outbreak on Santo, accessed on 03 October 2026. Other Information: One Health - High pathogenicity avian influenza • Australia: The national H5 bird flu dashboard recorded 682 confirmed events in wildlife as of 02 October 2026, against 652 on 25 September: South Australia 325, Victoria 230, New South Wales 68, Tasmania 46, Western Australia 10, Queensland 2 and Jervis Bay Territory 1. H5 has not been reported from any Pacific Island country or territory to date. – Source: Australian Government Department of Agriculture, Fisheries and Forestry, H5 bird flu latest data accessed on 05 October 2026. • Pacific Island countries and areas: The WHO Western Pacific Region Avian Influenza Weekly Update No. 1064 (02 October 2026) presents a rapid joint One Health risk assessment of high pathogenicity avian influenza incursion into Pacific Island countries and areas. Following detections of H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b in Australia and New Zealand, entry through seabird and wild bird movements in the south-west Pacific is assessed as very high risk from August 2026 to January 2027, and entry through poultry trade and human movement as low. If introduced, establishment would be high risk in resident wild birds and very high risk in domestic poultry, given free-ranging backyard poultry and limited biosecurity. Human exposure is assessed as low under current conditions. No human avian influenza case was reported in the Region. – Source: WHO Western Pacific Region, Avian Influenza Weekly Update No. 1064 accessed on 06 October 2026. Respiratory diseases • Guam: The Communicable Disease Surveillance Dashboard, updated on 05 October 2026, shows the late-season influenza wave reaching its highest level of 2026 in EpiWeek 38 with about 151 influenza and influenza-like illness cases, against about 121 the previous week. In EpiWeek 38, there were 62 laboratory-confirmed influenza A cases. All influenza viruses subtyped in August and September were A(H1N1) with 44 percent of cases are aged 5 to 19 years. – Source: Guam Department of Public Health and Social Services, Communicable Disease Surveillance Dashboard accessed on 06 October 2026. • Nauru: The Naoero Medical Centre has observed an ongoing increase in influenza infections, with more people seeking care for influenza-like symptoms, and health authorities urge the public to remain vigilant and take preventive measures. – Source: Nauru health authorities accessed on 06 October 2026. • Niue: The Public Health Snapshot for 22 to 28 September 2026 reports 17 new confirmed influenza A cases, after 24 the previous week, bringing the total to 104 since 17 August 2026. Thirteen hospital admissions, all for influenza A, have been recorded since 17 August. No COVID-19 or influenza B case was reported. – Source: Niue Public Health Division, Public Health Snapshot, 22-28 September 2026 accessed on 06 October 2026. • Palau: The Ministry of Health and Human Services reported on 02 October 2026 a total of 53 influenza A cases in 2026, with activity increasing in recent weeks. – Source: Palau Ministry of Health and Human Services, Respiratory Disease Situational Update accessed on 06 October 2026. • Tonga: The weekly syndromic surveillance report for EpiWeek 39 (21 to 27 September 2026) records 73 influenza-like illness cases, the fifth consecutive weekly increase and the highest weekly count of 2026, with a sharp rise in positive respiratory samples, mostly influenza A. On 05 October, the Ministry of Health issued an influenza A alert asking the public to watch for symptoms, with particular care for children under five, people aged 65 and over, pregnant women and heavy smokers. – Sources: Tonga Ministry of Health, Weekly Communicable Diseases Syndromic Surveillance, EpiWeek 39 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 01 October 2026 and Ministry of Health Tonga accessed on 06 October 2026. • Vanuatu: The Ministry of Health reported on 30 September 2026 that it is closely monitoring increased influenza-like illness activity, particularly in Shefa, Tafea and Penama provinces, where case numbers have remained above provincial alert thresholds in recent weeks. More than 19,000 ILI cases were reported nationally between EpiWeeks 1 and 37 of 2026. No outbreak has been declared. – Source: Vanuatu Ministry of Health, Health Promotion, ILI update accessed on 06 October 2026. • Wallis and Futuna: The Health Agency multi-source surveillance dashboard, updated on 05 October 2026, shows influenza-like illness rising steadily on Wallis since early September, reaching 69 consultations in the week ending of 21 September, the highest weekly level since January but below the alert threshold of 100. The Health Agency's update of 01 October 2026 reports one influenza A case on Wallis in week 39 and two on Futuna in week 40, while rhinovirus (5 cases) and parainfluenza virus 3 (4 cases) have circulated actively on Wallis since week 37, causing mild respiratory infections and bronchiolitis in children. No indicator is above the vigilance thresholds on either island, and increased vigilance for influenza-like illness is advised in the coming weeks. – Sources: Agence de Santé de Wallis et Futuna, multi-source surveillance dashboard accessed on 06 October 2026, and Agence de Santé de Wallis et Futuna, epidemiological and health watch update for weeks 37 to 39 (1st October 2026) shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 06 October 2026. Rotavirus • Tuvalu: Situational Report #9 on the rotavirus outbreak, covering 28 September to 04 October 2026, reports no new laboratory-confirmed case for the past four weeks. The cumulative total unchanged at 30, 28 on Funafuti and two on Nukufetau, the latest confirmed on 03 September. Genotyping confirmed an equine-like G3P[8] rotavirus. Two patients have been hospitalised and discharged, and 25 of the 30 cases are children under 15 years. A total of 389 suspected diarrhoea cases were reported between 04 August and 04 October, and weekly numbers have returned within the normal threshold. – Source: Tuvalu Ministry of Health, Outbreak of Rotavirus in Tuvalu, Situational Report #9 shared with the PPHSN Coordinating Body Focal point on 06 October 2026. • Wallis and Futuna: The Health Agency reported on 1st October 2026 that diarrhoeal syndromes on Wallis have been increa...
View five news elements
Time
The report was published on 6 October 2026. The event dates range from 15 August 2026 to 27 September 2026, with ongoing cases reported as of 1 October 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak is reported in the Marshall Islands (Majuro and Ebeye), Samoa, Vanuatu (Efate, Santo, Tafea Province, and West Tanna Area Council), and Wallis and Futuna. The affected population includes children under 15 years, who account for 73 percent of cases, and the general population in these regions.
Event
Dengue outbreaks were reported in the Marshall Islands, Samoa, Vanuatu, and Wallis and Futuna. The outbreaks are confirmed, with laboratory-confirmed cases and ongoing monitoring. The report also mentions a measles outbreak in Australia and a pertussis outbreak in Vanuatu, but these are not the primary focus of the analysis.
Scale, impact and risk
In the Marshall Islands, 112 confirmed cases were recorded, with no deaths and seven hospitalisations. In Samoa, 35 new cases were reported, a 169 percent increase from the previous week. Vanuatu reported 169 confirmed cases, with 16 hospitalisations and no deaths. Wallis and Futuna had 95 confirmed or probable cases. DENV-1 is the only serotype detected, and the red alert for dengue remains in effect.
Response, status and uncertainty
The outbreak in Vanuatu includes ongoing investigation, contact tracing, and community awareness efforts. The red alert for dengue remains in effect in the Marshall Islands, Samoa, Vanuatu, and Wallis and Futuna. The response status is under investigation, with no confirmed deaths or severe dengue cases reported.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

佛罗里达州正在看到 登革热 暴发。杜兰大学蚊子研究人员详细分析了病例上升情况 - 杜兰大学新闻

Florida is seeing a dengue fever outbreak. Tulane mosquito researcher breaks down the rise in cases - Tulane University News
新闻简报正如最近的新闻报道所强调的那样,佛罗里达州目前正在经历多年来最严重的 登革热 暴发 之一。美联社指出,这次 暴发 是美国大陆几十年来最大的一次,但没有提供具体病例或死亡人数。杜兰大学新闻报道了蚊子研究员 Dawn Wesson 的分析,他讨论了导致病例增加的因素。目前情况正在调查中,我们正在努力了解 暴发 的传播和影响。该报告没有提及寨卡病毒或西尼罗河病毒等相关病毒的任何确诊或疑似病例,这些病毒具有生物学相关性,但不是本次分析的主要焦点。该信息基于最近的观察,被认为是初步的,证据中没有发现硬性排除。

时间

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida is seeing a dengue fever outbreak. Tulane mosquito researcher breaks down the rise in cases - Tulane University News

News Brief
Florida is currently experiencing one of its most significant dengue fever outbreaks in years, as highlighted by recent news reports. The Associated Press noted that this outbreak is the largest in the continental US in decades, though specific case or death numbers were not provided. Tulane University News featured an analysis by mosquito researcher Dawn Wesson, who discussed the factors contributing to the rise in cases. The situation is under investigation, with ongoing efforts to understand the spread and impact of the outbreak. The report does not mention any confirmed or suspected cases of related viruses like Zika or West Nile, which are biologically related but not the primary focus of this analysis. The information is based on recent observations and is considered preliminary, with no hard exclusions identified in the evidence.

佛罗里达州与病例激增作斗争,坦帕妇女死于登革热 - CIDRAP

Tampa woman dies from dengue as Florida fights surge of cases - CIDRAP
新闻简报据 CIDRAP 称,随着佛罗里达州病例激增,坦帕市一名妇女死于登革热。该报告强调了登革热的严重性,它可能导致危及生命的并发症,例如登革出血热或登革休克综合征。虽然死亡得到确认,但没有提供更广泛的 暴发 的具体病例或死亡人数。CIDRAP 指出该事件是更大趋势的一部分,但没有包括调查、测试或遏制工作的详细信息。该报告强调了监测登革热 暴发 的重要性,特别是在病例不断增加的地区,以及采取公共卫生措施来减轻其传播和影响的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
儿科医生组织莱恩·伯杰森 (Laine Bergeson) 警告说,生奶会带来严重的感染风险,而且不会带来任何额外的健康益处。美国儿科学会 (AAP) 在一份声明中再次呼吁禁止销售和分销未经高温消毒的奶制品。
地点与对象
坦帕,佛罗里达州,美国。
事件
据 CIDRAP 报道,一名坦帕妇女死于登革热,这是佛罗里达州病例激增的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
据报道有 1 人死亡;没有提供佛罗里达州更广泛的登革热激增的具体病例或死亡人数。登革热是一种严重的疾病,可能会出现并发症,但没有明确的风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
CIDRAP 报告了这一事件,但没有提供有关调查、测试或遏制措施的具体细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Tampa woman dies from dengue as Florida fights surge of cases - CIDRAP

News Brief
A Tampa woman has died from dengue, as Florida experiences a surge in cases, according to CIDRAP. The report highlights the severity of dengue, which can lead to life-threatening complications such as dengue hemorrhagic fever or dengue shock syndrome. While the death is confirmed, no specific case or death counts for the broader outbreak were provided. CIDRAP noted the incident as part of a larger trend, but details on investigation, testing, or containment efforts were not included. The report underscores the importance of monitoring dengue outbreaks, particularly in regions with increasing cases, and the need for public health measures to mitigate its spread and impact.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

地图显示继佛罗里达州 暴发 之后 登革热 病例增加的州 - 新闻周刊

Map Shows States Where Dengue Fever Cases Have Risen After Florida Outbreak - Newsweek
新闻简报根据美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 的最新数据,继佛罗里达州 登革热 暴发(卫生官员称其为美国大陆数十年来最大的 登革热 暴发)之后,至少 15 个州报告的病例有所增加。佛罗里达州增幅最大,病例从 392 例上升至 485 例,增加了 93 例。加州增幅第二大,病例从 31 例上升至 45 例,增加了 14 例。各州报告的病例总数增加了 130 例。暴发 集中在坦帕湾地区,导致多个县宣布进入紧急状态。公共卫生专家将登革热活动的增加归因于国际旅行和可以传播病毒的伊蚊的存在。卫生官员建议居民使用美国环保署注册的驱虫剂、穿长袖衣服以及消除房屋周围的积水,以减少蚊子接触。《新闻周刊》已联系疾控中心征求意见,正在等待回复。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-10-04。事件日期:未指定。数据显示近期登革热病例有所增加。
地点与对象
美国,特别是佛罗里达州和其他州。暴发 集中在坦帕湾地区,其中佛罗里达州的病例数最多,其他州的病例数也有所增加。
事件
继佛罗里达州 暴发 疫情之后,美国至少 15 个州的 登革热 病例有所增加,卫生官员称这是数十年来最严重的一次。疾病预防控制中心报告病例增加。
规模、影响与风险
至少有 15 个州报告的登革热病例有所增加。佛罗里达州有 485 例病例,比 392 例增加了 93 例。加州有 45 例,从 31 例增加了 14 例。各州新增病例总数为 130 例。由于本地 传播 和 重症登革热 的潜力,风险会升高。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员敦促居民减少与蚊子的接触。疾病预防控制中心正在监测情况并已发布最新情况。《新闻周刊》已联系疾控中心征求意见,正在等待回复。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Map Shows States Where Dengue Fever Cases Have Risen After Florida Outbreak - Newsweek

News Brief
Following the dengue fever outbreak in Florida, which health officials describe as the largest in the continental United States in decades, reported cases have increased in at least 15 states, according to the latest data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). Florida accounted for the largest share of the increase, with cases rising from 392 to 485, an increase of 93 cases. California recorded the second-largest increase, with cases rising from 31 to 45, an increase of 14 cases. The total rise in reported cases across the states was 130. The outbreak has been concentrated in the Tampa Bay region, leading to emergency declarations in several counties. Public health experts attribute the rise in dengue activity to international travel and the presence of Aedes mosquitoes, which can transmit the virus. Health officials are advising residents to reduce mosquito exposure by using EPA-registered insect repellents, wearing long sleeves, and eliminating standing water around homes. Newsweek has contacted the CDC for comment and is awaiting a response.
Original source text (English)
Uncommon Knowledge Memo To America: This Is What a Crisis Looks Like U.S. News Politics AI Politics Tech Fact Check Sports Global Iran War Russia-Ukraine Middle East China And Asia Live Blog Better Planet All World News Lifestyle Family & Parenting Entertainment Travel Pets Automotive Relationships Science Health Personal Finance Better Workplaces Readers Choice Opinion My Turn All Opinion Rankings Health Finance Products Education Workplaces Corporate Responsibility All Rankings Watch The 1600 The Royal Report Unconventional From The Paddock Newsmakers Extra Newsletters Dare to Dream Events Essays Podcasts Vantage Insights Magazine CEO Circle Announcements Leadership Dev Pragad Subscribe Sign In U.S. NewsPoliticsAI PoliticsTechFact CheckSports Global Iran WarRussia-UkraineMiddle EastChina And AsiaLive BlogBetter PlanetAll World News Lifestyle Family & ParentingEntertainmentTravelPetsAutomotiveRelationshipsScienceHealthPersonal FinanceBetter WorkplacesReaders Choice Opinion My TurnAll Opinion Rankings HealthCorporate ResponsibilityFinanceProductsEducationWorkAll Rankings Watch The 1600The Royal ReportUnconventionalFrom The PaddockNewsmakers Social FacebookInstagramYouTubeRedditTikTokTwitter/X Extra NewslettersDare to DreamEventsEssaysPodcastsVantageInsightsMagazineCEO CircleAnnouncementsLeadershipDev Pragad Following the dengue fever outbreak in Florida, which health officials are describing as the largest outbreak in the continental United States in decades, dengue fever cases have increased across a number of U.S. states in recent days, according to the latest data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). The data shows that the number of reported cases has risen in at least 15 states, with Florida accounting for the largest share of the increase. Florida Outbreak Continues to Grow The rise in national case numbers comes as Florida deals with an ongoing dengue outbreak that has drawn attention from public health officials. Florida has recorded hundreds of dengue infections this year, including a significant number of locally acquired cases, meaning people were infected within the state rather than while traveling abroad. The outbreak has been concentrated in the Tampa Bay region and has resulted in emergency declarations in several counties. What Is Dengue Fever? Dengue is a viral disease transmitted by infected mosquitoes. According to the CDC, symptoms can include fever, headache, pain behind the eyes, nausea, vomiting, rash, muscle pain, and joint pain. Most infections are mild, and many people develop no symptoms at all. However, some patients can develop severe dengue, a potentially life-threatening condition that can cause shock, severe bleeding, and organ failure. Why Are Cases Increasing? Public health experts have linked rising dengue activity in the United States to several factors, including international travel and the presence of Aedes mosquitoes, which are capable of transmitting the virus. Dengue is common in many tropical and subtropical regions. When travelers return to the United States while infected, local mosquito populations can sometimes spread the virus to other people. CDC Data Show Increases Across Multiple States The latest CDC data show reported case counts have risen since the agency's previous update. Florida posted by far the largest increase, with cases rising from 392 to 485, an increase of 93 cases. California recorded the second-largest increase, rising from 31 to 45 cases, an increase of 14. Other states showing increases were: Overall, the states listed in the CDC updates recorded a rise of 130 cases between the reporting periods. What Happens Next? Health officials continue to urge residents to reduce mosquito exposure by using EPA-registered insect repellents, wearing long sleeves, and eliminating standing water around homes where mosquitoes can breed. The latest CDC data suggest dengue activity remains elevated in Florida and is continuing to increase in a number of other states. While most U.S. cases are associated with travel, the Florida outbreak has demonstrated how quickly local transmission can develop when the virus is introduced into areas where dengue-carrying mosquitoes are present. Scale, impact and risk
At least 15 states have seen an increase in reported dengue cases. Florida had 485 cases, up from 392, an increase of 93 cases. California had 45 cases, up from 31, an increase of 14 cases. The total rise across states was 130 cases. The risk is elevated due to local transmission and potential for severe dengue.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials are urging residents to reduce mosquito exposure. The CDC is monitoring the situation and has issued updates. Newsweek has contacted the CDC for comment and is awaiting a response.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

What to Know About the Dengue Outbreak in the U.S. - Time Magazine

News Brief
A dengue outbreak has been reported in Hillsborough County, Florida, with a state of emergency declared this week. According to the CDC, around 485 cases have been recorded in the state this year, and one person has died from the disease. The outbreak involves locally acquired infections, which is unusual for the U.S., as most cases typically result from travel-associated exposure. Dengue is transmitted by Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, and the risk of severe illness, including dengue hemorrhagic fever, is higher for individuals who have been previously infected. Public health officials are urging residents to take preventive measures, such as avoiding mosquito bites and eliminating breeding sites, to curb the spread. While cooler weather may reduce transmission, the situation remains under investigation, and vigilance is still necessary.
Original source text (English)
The Brief Daily · The day's top stories, summarized for you. Thank you for signing up! This week, Hillsborough County in Florida, which includes Tampa, declared a state of emergency linked to a growing number of dengue cases. So far this year, around 485 cases have been reported in the state, according to the CDC. The disease, which is transmitted by mosquitoes, can cause serious flu-like illness, and one person in Florida has died in the outbreak so far this year, the state’s department of health told media outlets in September. Seasonal outbreaks of the disease, which affects up to 400 million people worldwide annually, are not unusual in Florida, and they’re often linked to travelers. But this time is a little different, says Michael Johansson, an epidemiologist at Northeastern University. “This is the most locally acquired cases that we've seen in the state,” he says. What is dengue? Dengue, sometimes called dengue fever, is caused by a collection of viruses carried by mosquitoes, primarily Aedes aegypti. Dengue is endemic in a wide swath of the tropics, and usually, most Americans who have the infection acquire it outside of the U.S. during travel. Dengue symptoms can occur 5-7 days after being bitten and range from mild to severe. They may include painful body aches as well as fever and can last up to 10 days. However, in parts of the U.S. where Aedes aegypti mosquitoes live, they can bite infected people and start to spread the disease locally. “The general pattern that we see—and we've seen this for more than a decade now—is that there are frequent travel-associated cases” in Florida, says Johansson. “We know we have the right vectors there, especially in southern Florida, where there's lots of Aedes aegypti, and there is some local transmission that occurs. Usually that looks like just one or two cases, but sometimes there are small outbreaks.” In 2009, when two locally acquired infections were identified in Key West, public-health surveillance revealed that a substantial share of people in the town had markers in their blood showing a recent encounter with dengue, even if they hadn’t had symptoms. That suggests that in an outbreak, dengue is likely to be infecting more people than might immediately be clear from case counts. One distinctive feature of dengue, however, is that the second time someone is infected is likely to be much worse than the first. Sometimes a second infection can progress to dengue hemorrhagic fever, which can involve bleeding and a drop in blood pressure, and is sometimes fatal. This pattern, where the second exposure is more dangerous than the first, has complicated attempts to vaccinate people against the disease. In 2017, Sanofi Pasteur, the manufacturer of the dengue vaccine Dengvaxia, alerted the government of the Philippines that serious dengue infections might occur in people who had received the vaccine as their first exposure to the disease. No dengue vaccine is currently available in the U.S.; Dengvaxia was available starting in 2019 but in 2025 was discontinued by the manufacturer, which cited low demand. Will dengue become endemic in the U.S.? So far, dengue has not established a permanent foothold in the continental U.S., says Johansson. There are a few reasons why that might be: large numbers of people in Florida, where Aedes aegypti thrive, stay within air-conditioned spaces when they’re most active, which means chains of transmission tend to be short. And the mosquitoes themselves are not particularly adventurous. They tend to stay close to home, within about a 100-meter range, so “it takes people to move the virus around,” says Johansson. If someone who’s infected doesn’t travel, the virus doesn’t either. How can you protect yourself from dengue? There is no cure for dengue; the only treatments are for symptoms. If you are in an area with mosquitoes that can carry dengue, the best way to protect yourself is to avoid mosquito bites. Keep windows closed or use screens, cover as much of your skin as possible with clothing when outdoors, and use mosquito repellent, says Johansson. Keep yards free of containers that could fill with water and serve as breeding zones for mosquitoes. You’ll have to be vigilant: “These mosquitoes in particular, they can breed in a plastic bag that's collected a little bit of rainwater,” says Johansson. Cooler weather brings relief. “We're getting to the time of year when things are cooling down, so we should expect a decrease in transmission,” says Johansson. “However, it's still hot in Florida right now”—so it’s not time to drop your guard yet
View five news elements

The outbreak in Hillsborough County, Florida, has been ongoing this year, with a state of emergency declared this week. The CDC reported 485 cases so far this year, and one death was confirmed in September.
Location and population
The outbreak is centered in Hillsborough County, Florida, which includes Tampa. The affected population includes residents of the county, particularly those in areas with Aedes aegypti mosquitoes.
Event
Hillsborough County in Florida declared a state of emergency due to a growing number of dengue cases. The outbreak involves locally acquired infections, which is unusual for the U.S. and indicates potential for increased transmission.
Scale, impact and risk
As of the current year, 485 cases have been reported in Florida, with one death linked to the outbreak. The risk of severe dengue, including dengue hemorrhagic fever, is noted, especially for individuals who have been previously infected.
Response, status and uncertainty
Public health officials are monitoring the outbreak, emphasizing the need for vigilance and prevention measures. The CDC and state health departments are providing guidance on avoiding mosquito bites and reducing breeding sites. The situation remains under investigation, and the risk of further spread is acknowledged.

Dengue fever outbreak spreads to 252 cases across Florida, including SWFL - WINK News

News Brief
WINK News reported on October 6, 2026, that a dengue fever outbreak has spread to 252 cases across Florida, particularly in Southwest Florida. The outbreak is linked to Tropical Storm Isaias, which is expected to strengthen in the Gulf and bring heavy rainfall and scattered thunderstorms. While the specific dengue virus type (DENV-1 to DENV-4) was not confirmed, the implications of the outbreak suggest that low vaccination rates and Florida’s aging population may contribute to its spread. Local authorities and crews are preparing for potential impacts from the storm, though no details on testing, isolation, or contact tracing were provided. The report highlights the need for vigilance and preparedness in the region as the storm approaches.

蚊子血统决定了整个地中海的基孔肯雅热和登革热风险

Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean
新闻简报发表在 Parasites & 媒介 上的一项新研究表明,地中海白纹伊蚊种群的遗传血统显着影响其传播基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 和 登革病毒 (DENV) 的能力。该研究由 Riccardo Piccinno 和 Anna Rodolfa Malacrida 领导,结合群体遗传学和实验性 媒介 能力测定,以评估遗传谱系如何影响病毒 媒介。研究结果表明,具有不同遗传背景的邻近蚊子种群可能具有截然不同的能力特征,而地理上相距遥远的共享祖先的种群则表现出相似的 传播 潜力。这种由混乱的引入和混合事件形成的遗传异质性表明,欧洲的 媒介 控制策略应根据当地蚊子种群量身定制,而不是统一应用。该研究强调了了解蚊子来源作为风险评估的一部分以及昆虫学 监测 和病例监测的重要性,以更好地预测虫媒病毒 监测。所提供的证据中没有报告具体的病例数或死亡人数。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-10-07。事件日期:未在提供的证据中报告。时间范围:未在提供的证据中报告。
地点与对象
地区:地中海盆地,包括瑞士南部、意大利北部和中部、巴尔干半岛和希腊。种群:白纹伊蚊种群。
事件
发表在 Parasites & 媒介 上的一项研究发现,地中海白纹伊蚊种群的遗传血统显着影响其传播基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 和 登革病毒 (DENV) 的能力。这一发现基于群体遗传学和实验 媒介 能力测定。研究结果证实了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
该研究发现不同蚊子种群的 媒介 能力存在显着差异,一些祖先分数显示与 CHIKV 和 DENV 的感染和 传播 率呈正相关。所提供的证据中没有报告具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露人数。明确指出风险是由遗传血统而不是地理邻近性决定的。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究的结果得到了证实,作者指出,遗传血统和群体身份显着影响 媒介 能力。该研究涉及群体遗传学和实验分析,结果得到统计分析的支持。该研究强调需要局部 媒介 控制策略,但提供的证据中没有报告具体的应对措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean

News Brief
A new study published in Parasites & Vectors reveals that the genetic ancestry of Aedes albopictus mosquito populations in the Mediterranean significantly influences their ability to transmit chikungunya virus (CHIKV) and dengue virus (DENV). The research, led by Riccardo Piccinno and Anna Rodolfa Malacrida, combined population genetics and experimental vector competence assays to assess how genetic lineages affect viral transmission. The findings show that neighboring mosquito populations with different genetic backgrounds can have drastically different competence profiles, while geographically distant populations sharing ancestry display similar transmission potential. This genetic heterogeneity, shaped by chaotic introductions and admixture events, suggests that vector control strategies in Europe should be tailored to local mosquito populations rather than applied uniformly. The study underscores the importance of understanding mosquito provenance as part of risk assessment, alongside entomological surveillance and case monitoring, to better anticipate arboviral outbreaks. No specific case counts or death numbers were reported in the supplied evidence.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, October 7, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean by October 7, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram The Asian tiger mosquito, Aedes albopictus, has earned its reputation as one of the world’s most hazardous invasive species. Since first establishing itself in the Mediterranean Basin in the early 1980s, it has spread across southern Europe and become increasingly implicated in local, or autochthonous, transmission of chikungunya and dengue viruses in regions where these diseases were once exclusively travel-associated. A new study published in Parasites & Vectors now shows that the mosquito’s tangled colonization history is not just a curiosity of population genetics: the genetic ancestry of individual Mediterranean populations significantly shapes how competently they can acquire, disseminate and transmit chikungunya virus (CHIKV), dengue virus (DENV) and Zika virus (ZIKV), effectively redrawing the map of arboviral risk across Europe. The research, led by Riccardo Piccinno and Anna Rodolfa Malacrida of the University of Pavia, together with colleagues at the Institut Pasteur in Paris and partners in Switzerland, Italy and the Balkans, set out to answer a deceptively simple question: do genetically distinct mosquito populations differ in their ability to transmit arboviruses? The answer, the authors report, is a clear yes, and the pattern they uncovered is striking. Neighboring mosquito populations with different genetic ancestries showed drastically different competence profiles for CHIKV and DENV, while geographically distant populations that share ancestry behaved similarly. In other words, for these viruses, where a mosquito’s genes came from matters more than where the mosquito lives. To reach that conclusion, the team combined two complementary lines of evidence: population genetics and experimental vector competence assays. On the genetic side, they genotyped mosquitoes from an ancestral reference population in Guangzhou, China, and from ten adventive populations sampled across five Mediterranean regions, spanning Southern Switzerland, Northern and Central Italy, the Balkans and Greece. The markers of choice were highly polymorphic simple sequence repeats, or SSRs, which allow fine-scale discrimination of genetic lineages. Using the Bayesian clustering program STRUCTURE, the researchers assigned individuals to ancestry groups, and with the approximate Bayesian computation framework DIYABC-RF they reconstructed the demographic histories that produced the present-day genetic mosaic. That history, the study finds, was anything but a single clean invasion wave. By combining historical records with demographic modeling, the authors show that the establishment of Aedes albopictus in the Mediterranean was driven by chaotic, discontinuous introductions of distinct lineages, followed by admixture events in which previously separated genetic backgrounds interbred. The result is high heterogeneity in both space and ancestry: populations separated by only a few hundred kilometers can carry markedly different genetic compositions, reflecting separate introduction routes, likely via the international trade in used tires and other commodities that has repeatedly ferried mosquito eggs across borders. This genetic patchwork, rather than a homogeneous expansion front, is the biological backdrop against which viral transmission now plays out. The experimental half of the study measured vector competence using the standard trio of quantitative indices. Infection rate captures the proportion of mosquitoes in which the virus successfully establishes an infection in the midgut after an infectious blood meal; dissemination efficiency measures how many of those infected mosquitoes allow the virus to escape the midgut and spread to secondary organs, including the salivary glands; and transmission efficiency quantifies the fraction with virus present in the saliva, the prerequisite for onward transmission to a new host. Mosquitoes from each population were fed blood meals containing CHIKV strain 06.21, DENV-1, or ZIKV strain PE243, and these indices were scored at multiple days post-infection, specifically 7, 14 and 21 days, to track the temporal progression of infection. Statistical analysis then linked the genetic and virological datasets. Univariate and multivariate models assessed whether population identity, ancestry group membership, and days post-infection were associated with each competence index. For CHIKV, the analyses identified day post-infection, population, and two ancestry components as significant factors associated with infection, with some ancestry fractions showing positive associations and others negative ones. For DENV, population and three ancestry components were significantly associated with infection rate, although the authors note that two of these lacked a biologically plausible monotonic trend across ancestry categories, meaning higher ancestry fractions did not simply translate into higher infection rates. For ZIKV, by contrast, no variable emerged as a significant predictor, suggesting that the genetic heterogeneity captured in this study does not translate into measurable variation in Zika competence among these populations. The most consequential finding concerns CHIKV and DENV. Ancestry and population identity significantly influenced dissemination and transmission as well as infection, and the geographic pattern was counterintuitive: proximity did not predict similarity. Populations in neighboring geographical areas but with distinct ancestries exhibited drastically different competence profiles, whereas geographically distant populations sharing genetic ancestry displayed similar vector competence. This dissociation between geography and genetics implies that the introduction history of each local population, not local environmental conditions alone, has left a durable imprint on its capacity to transmit these viruses. It also suggests that the genetic variants governing viral infection and dissemination vary among source lineages, so that admixture has produced a mosaic of transmission potential across the region. The public health implications are direct. Europe has experienced a rising trend of both imported and autochthonous arboviral infections in recent years, with chikungunya and dengue outbreaks recorded in Italy, France and elsewhere. If the mosquitoes buzzing in one province are substantially more competent vectors than those in the next, then uniform, region-wide control assumptions may misallocate resources. The authors argue that their results underscore the urgent need for localized, geographically tailored vector control strategies in Europe, in which surveillance and intervention intensity are calibrated to the actual transmission potential of local mosquito populations rather than to a generic regional estimate. Methodologically, the study demonstrates the value of integrating fine-scale population genomics with standardized vector competence assays. The SSR-based genotyping, combined with STRUCTURE clustering and DIYABC-RF demographic inference, allowed the team to move beyond simple labels of origin and to quantify ancestry fractions within admixed populations. The logistic regression framework then made it possible to test the contribution of each factor while accounting for others, including the strong temporal effects of days post-infection that are well known to shape competence measurements. Supplementary analyses reported pairwise genetic differentiation among populations and detailed infection, dissemination and transmission rates stratified by virus, time point and population, providing a transparent record of the underlying data. The work also carries broader lessons for invasion biology. Aedes albopictus is a textbook example of how global trade creates repeated, genetically diverse introductions, and this study shows that such demographic chaos can have functional consequences for disease transmission. As the climate warms and the mosquito’s suitable habitat expands northward, new introductions and further admixture are likely, potentially reshaping vector competence in currently lower-risk areas. Understanding the provenance of each new population, the authors suggest, should therefore become part of the risk-assessment toolkit, alongside entomological surveillance and case monitoring, if Europe is to anticipate rather than merely react to the next arboviral emergence. Subject of Research: Vector competence and population genetics of invasive Aedes albopictus mosquitoes in the Mediterranean Basin Article Title: Provenance and competence for CHIKV and DENV of Aedes albopictus populations Article References: Piccinno, R., Madec, Y., Mariconti, M., Fiorenza, G., Carraretto, D., Forneris, F., Flacio, E., Coletti, S., Gasperi, G., Failloux, A.-B., & Malacrida, A. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida's dengue fever outbreak passes 250 cases, majority in Hillsborough County - wtsp.com

News Brief
Florida's dengue fever outbreak has surpassed 250 cases, with the majority reported in Hillsborough County. The outbreak is confirmed by local news reports, and the Tampa mayor has warned that the response could be limited if Amendment 3 passes. The evidence does not provide a specific death count, but the outbreak is under investigation. Prevention measures, including clearing standing water, are being considered. The situation remains uncertain, with no explicit risk level stated in the provided information. The outbreak is part of a broader public health concern, and the response is being evaluated for its effectiveness and limitations.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

佛罗里达州登革热 暴发 发出长期病毒威胁的信号

Florida Dengue Outbreak Signals Longer-Term Viral Threat
新闻简报佛罗里达州本地获得的登革热病例大幅增加,截至 2026 年第 38 周(9 月 20 日至 26 日)报告了 252 例,而 2025 年为 40 例,2024 年为 37 例。这标志着与往年大多数病例是旅行获得的情况的转变。暴发 包括一例确诊死亡病例,可能与登革热重复感染有关。佛罗里达州卫生部确认坦帕所在的希尔斯伯勒县出现首例登革热相关死亡病例。专家警告说,抗体依赖性增强 (ADE) 导致后续感染可能导致严重并发症,包括器官衰竭和死亡。州官员正在加强蚊虫控制工作,例如消除积水,以减轻蚊子传播。然而,登革热 疫苗 在美国并未广泛使用,监管障碍推迟了其批准。虽然武田的 Qdenga 疫苗 已在包括巴西和欧盟在内的 40 多个国家获得批准,但在美国仍未获得批准。赛诺菲的 Dengvaxia 疫苗 于 2019 年获得批准,由于需求低迷而于 2023 年停产。这种情况凸显了佛罗里达州需要提高公众意识和有针对性的预防策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 的报告截至 2026 年第 38 周(9 月 20 日至 26 日),并于 9 月确认死亡。2024 年全球 暴发 报告的病例截至当年第 38 周。
地点与对象
暴发 正在佛罗里达州发生,特别是在坦帕所在的希尔斯伯勒县。受影响的人群包括佛罗里达州的居民,特别是那些有积水、蚊子可以繁殖的地区的居民。
事件
佛罗里达州当地报告的 传播 登革热病例激增,其中一人死亡。州和县官员证实了这一事件,死亡事件与重复登革热感染有关。
规模、影响与风险
As of the 38th week of 2026, there were 252 reported cases of locally acquired dengue in Florida, compared to 40 cases in 2025 and 37 in 2024. The 2024 global outbreak had over 14.6 million cases and more than 6000 deaths. The risk of subsequent infections is highlighted due to antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE), which can lead to severe complications and even death.
应对、状态与不确定性
佛罗里达州和县官员正在加紧努力,消灭传播登革热的蚊子。公共卫生措施包括排空房屋周围的积水和提高认识。佛罗里达州卫生部确认希尔斯伯勒县出现首例登革热相关死亡病例。登革热 疫苗 在美国的供应有限,将 Qdenga 和 Dengvaxia 推向市场的努力面临着监管挑战。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida Dengue Outbreak Signals Longer-Term Viral Threat

News Brief
Florida is experiencing a significant surge in locally acquired dengue cases, with 252 reported as of the 38th week of 2026 (September 20-26), compared to 40 in 2025 and 37 in 2024. This marks a shift from previous years where most cases were travel-acquired. The outbreak includes one confirmed fatality, potentially linked to a repeat dengue infection. The Florida Department of Health has confirmed the first dengue-related death in Hillsborough County, where Tampa is located. Experts warn that subsequent infections can lead to severe complications, including organ failure and death, due to antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE). State officials are intensifying mosquito control efforts, such as eliminating standing water, to mitigate the spread. However, dengue vaccines are not widely available in the US, with regulatory hurdles delaying their approval. While Takeda’s Qdenga vaccine has been approved in over 40 countries, including Brazil and the EU, it remains unapproved in the US. Sanofi’s Dengvaxia vaccine, approved in 2019, was discontinued in 2023 due to low demand. The situation underscores the need for improved public awareness and targeted prevention strategies in Florida.
Original source text (English)
Your Clinical Hub News & Perspective Tools & Reference CME/CE More Video Events Specialties Topics RegisterLog In Medscape Medical NewsFeatures Florida Dengue Outbreak Signals Longer-Term Viral Threat Kerry Dooley Young October 07, 2026 Florida is experiencing a surge of reported cases of local transmission of dengue, including one fatality, with experts seeing longer-term trends likely to increase the global spread of this viral infection. As of the 38th week of this year (September 20-26), there were 252 reported cases of locally acquired dengue in Florida, compared with 40 cases and 37 cases at this point, respectively, in 2025 and 2024. This pattern is the reverse of what has happened in recent years with Florida’s cases of travel-acquired dengue. Given Florida’s close ties to the Caribbean and South America, where dengue is more prevalent, the state has long seen cases of people experiencing infections acquired abroad. During the historic record global outbreak in 2024 of more than 14.6 million cases reported worldwide, travel-acquired dengue soared in Florida. Reported cases in the state hit 539 as of the 38th week of 2024, up from 316 by that point in the previous year. The equivalent number for this year is 245 travel-related cases. With a spike in locally acquired cases this year, state and county officials in Florida are stepping up efforts to combat the mosquitoes that spread the disease. Emptying standing water around the home is critical to reducing growth of the mosquito population, said Kristi Miley, PhD, a researcher at the University of South Florida College of Public Health Center for Global Health and Infectious Disease Research in Tampa, Florida. She advises people to check places like flowerpots, children’s toys, buckets, gutters, and old tires. At this time, there is not a dengue vaccine available in the US. There are several reasons for this, including dengue’s historic relatively low prevalence nationwide. In a nation like Brazil, though, there’s a critical need for a vaccine, Miley said. In the 2024 outbreak, more than 10 million dengue cases were reported, including more than 6000 deaths. “Brazil is a community that definitely can benefit from a vaccine because they probably have a large proportion of the population that’s already been infected” with at least one kind of dengue, said Miley. Initial dengue infections tend to be mild, with symptoms like headaches, if people have any symptoms. An estimated 40%-80% of infections are asymptomatic. Many people are unaware they have had a dengue infection. But subsequent dengue infections can cause serious complications, including organ failure and even in rare cases, death. The danger of subsequent infections stems from the challenges for the human immune system in fighting a virus with four closely related serotypes: DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4. Researchers use the familiar concept of the Trojan horse as a way to explain why subsequent infections with dengue tend to be more serious than initial ones, Miley said. An initial infection triggers the production of antibodies that would work well against that type of dengue. But these antibodies do not neutralize other kinds of dengue virus. What then happens is a phenomenon that scientists call antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE). Instead of protecting against other strains, ADE allows the dengue virus easier access into immune cells and spurs more inflammation. “You’re actually opening the door and letting it invade,” Miley said. News accounts indicate that it may have been a repeat dengue infection that led to the death of 80-year-old Gay Small of Tampa, Florida, in September. Her daughter, Anna Small, spoke with WFLA news, saying that her mother had traveled extensively earlier in her life. She may have had dengue previously without knowing it, Small said. “This virus just kind of comes out of nowhere,” Small told the news station. “She met the wrong mosquito, and that’s it.” In her younger years, Gay Small traveled with her husband to places like Asia and South America for his work, which included efforts to fight infectious diseases, Small told the Tampa Bay Times. Small said she spoke with the media about her mother’s case to make people more aware of the risk for dengue in Florida. “If she can let people know the severity of this,” she told the Tampa Bay Times, “maybe it will give her death meaning.” The Florida Department of Health has confirmed for media organizations, including Medscape Medical News, that there has been a first confirmed dengue-related death in Hillsborough County, Florida, where Tampa is located. Sanofi Withdraws, Takeda Expands There have been both notable advances and setbacks in the past decade with efforts to bring dengue vaccines to market. Three products have been approved by regulators, with only one broadly available globally now, but not including the US. Japan’s Takeda Pharmaceutical has secured approval of its two-dose Qdenga dengue vaccine in more than 40 countries, including India in July 2026. The European Union’s pharmaceutical regulator cleared it in 2022. Brazilian approval followed in 2023. The US remains an exception. Takeda in 2023 voluntarily withdrew its application for FDA approval of its Qdenga vaccine, citing disagreements about “data collection.” Takeda told Medscape Medical News that there are no immediate or additional actions planned to resume efforts to win US approval of Qdenga, but the company has continued a dialogue with the agency. The FDA did earlier clear a different dengue vaccine for limited use in the US, but it’s no longer marketed. The FDA in May 2019 approved Dengvaxia from France’s Sanofi. It was intended for people aged 9-16 years who lived in areas where dengue is endemic, such as Puerto Rico, who had a laboratory-documented record of a prior infection with this virus. France’s Sanofi in 2023 decided to discontinue Dengvaxia. In an email, Sanofi told Medscape Medical News that the reason was “low global demand for this product,” and not quality, safety, or efficacy concerns. Dengvaxia arrived at the market with high hopes. In an announcement about the 2015 Mexican approval of the vaccine, Sanofi described it as “a historic milestone for our company, for the global public health community and, most importantly, for half the world’s population who lives at risk of dengue.” But concerns arose in the Philippines about deaths of children after Dengvaxia. Data indicated that the vaccine raised the risk for future life-threatening illness in those who had not been exposed to the virus. The government of the Philippines pulled the vaccine’s license in 2019. The third vaccine to win a major regulatory clearance is a one-dose product, which the National Institutes of Health (NIH) helped develop with partners including Brazil’s Instituto Butantan. Brazil last year cleared this vaccine, known as Butantan-DV, an achievement that NIH described as a “quarter century of hard work” paying off. But a Butantan-DV vaccination campaign within Brazil’s public health system was suspended in June. Brazilian health officials are examining 42 cases of adverse events, including two deaths, among people who were vaccinated. The Brazilian Health Regulatory Agency (Agência Nacional de Vigilância Sanitária) said in June that a panel of experts would aid in the investigation of whether these reported side effects are linked to Butantan-DV. The halt with the vaccination campaign does not mean work on the Butantan-DV vaccine had stopped in Brazil. A study of the Butantan-DV vaccine in older people (NCT06891950) continues in Brazil, a Butantan spokesperson told Medscape Medical News. More Than 32 Million Doses Takeda also continues to study the safety and effectiveness of Qdenga post-approval. This work includes a study designed to provide more information on its effectiveness against DEN-3 and DEN-4, which are the least common dengue virus serotypes. There’s been fairly broad uptake of the vaccine, with Takeda saying more than 32 million doses of Qdenga have been distributed. As published in The New England Journal of Medicine in 2019, earlier results from a pivotal study on Qdenga, known as TIDES, suggested the vaccine worked well against DENV-1 and DENV-2, while raising concerns about its effectiveness against the other two types of the virus. So far, these hypothetical safety concerns have not emerged, said Stephen J. Thomas, MD, a professor of microbiology and immunology and public health and preventive medicine at the SUNY Upstate Medical University in Syracuse, New York. Thomas has studied dengue for more than 25 years, having earlier worked at the Walter Reed Army Institute of Research. Thomas noted that Takeda must track and report data on how people have fared after Qdenga vaccination. Company officials must watch for reports of severe reactions and for evidence the vaccine is putting recipients at greater risk for disease or severe disease than people who are not vaccinated, he said. “So it’s not like people aren’t looking. People are looking” for evidence of safety concerns, Thomas said. “It just hasn’t really manifested itself.” “Is Takeda’s vaccine a home run? Absolutely not,” Thomas said. “But it does seem to be safe across different ages and different exposure histories and is delivering a public health benefit.” Rising Concern About 5.6 billion people now live in areas with a risk for dengue, with an estimated 100-400 million infections occurring each year, according to the World Health Organization (WHO). This risk for dengue is likely to increase due to climate change. Expected warmer weather and other factors likely will aid the spread of the mosquitoes that transmit the virus. For example, research suggests the ranges for dengue potentially could increase in North America, Asia, Europe, and sub-Saharan Africa to put another 2.25 billion people at risk for the infection, according to a 2022 report from the United Nations’ Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change. “Dengue is no longer a sporadic threat confined to the tropics. It is the mosquito-borne virus with the highest epidemic and pandemic potential,” WHO Director Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus, PhD, MSc, in a June statement. “Outbreaks are increasing in frequency, scale, and geographic reach, driven by urbanization, climate change, population movement, and fragile health systems.” Miley reported no financial conflicts. Thomas reported being a consultant, advisory board member, or blinded case adjudication committee member for GlaxoSmithKline, Sanofi, Takeda, Merck, and Codagenix. He has a patent related to dengue vaccines and all rights were assigned to the US government. Kerry Dooley Young is a journalist based in Washington, DC. She has reported on health care for more than 20 years, including in-depth coverage of the FDA and the pharmaceutical industry. Comments Commenting is limited to medical professionals. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

由于当地登革热创纪录,佛罗里达州各县宣布进入紧急状态

Florida Counties Declare Emergencies As Local Dengue Hits A Record
新闻简报包括希尔斯伯勒在内的佛罗里达州三个县因登革热 暴发 宣布进入紧急状态,自 2026 年 7 月以来已有 252 例当地感染病例。由埃及伊蚊推动的 暴发 导致采取了紧急措施和持续的蚊虫控制工作。已报告一例死亡,病毒来源和传播情况正在调查中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
照片中展示的是一只正在休息的成年雌性埃及伊蚊(也称为黄热病蚊子),摄于 2022 年。
地点与对象
佛罗里达州,特别是希尔斯伯勒县、皮内拉斯县和帕斯科县。
事件
佛罗里达州的三个县因登革热 监测 宣布进入紧急状态,自 2026 年 7 月以来已有 252 例本地感染病例,比现代 监测 开始以来的任何一年都要多。
规模、影响与风险
252 例本地感染病例,1 例死亡,并在拥有 300 万人口的都市区持续传播。暴发 是由埃及伊蚊推动的,这些蚊子在私人财产中繁殖,导致蚊子控制工作的加强。
应对、状态与不确定性
希尔斯伯勒县、皮尼拉斯县和帕斯科县已将紧急命令延长至 10 月 8 日,措施包括暂停合同、采购和雇用的常规规则,以及授权工作人员在不另行通知的情况下进入私人土地。暴发 正在接受调查,正在进行蚊子控制工作和测试以确定病毒的来源和传播。

Florida Counties Declare Emergencies As Local Dengue Hits A Record

News Brief
Three Florida counties, including Hillsborough, declared states of emergency due to a dengue outbreak, with 252 locally acquired cases since July 2026. The outbreak, fueled by Aedes aegypti mosquitoes, has led to emergency measures and ongoing mosquito control efforts. One death has been reported, and the source and spread of the virus are under investigation.
Original source text (English)
A resting adult female Aedes aegypti mosquito, also known as the yellow fever mosquito, is shown, 2022. Aedes aegypti is fueling the ongoing outbreak of dengue in Florida. Image courtesy CDC. (Photo by Smith Collection/Gado/Getty Images) Three counties around Tampa Bay are under states of emergency over a virus that, until this summer, almost nobody there caught at home. Florida has recorded 252 cases of locally acquired dengue since July, more than in any year since the state's modern surveillance began. Hillsborough County, home to Tampa, accounts for 223 of them. Before this year it had recorded two local cases in a decade . The count was still rising at last report, with 62 new cases in the final full week of September. An 80-year-old Tampa woman bitten near her home on Bayshore Boulevard died on September 4 . On Monday, Broward County confirmed its first local case of the year, the eighth Florida county with local transmission in 2026. "Locally acquired" is the phrase that matters. Most dengue diagnosed in the United States is caught abroad and carried home. In 2024, a record year for US dengue, 97% of cases were travel-associated and only 105 were acquired locally, across three states. A local case means a Florida mosquito bit an infected person in Florida and passed the virus to someone else. Florida has now logged more than twice the country's entire 2024 local total, most of it in one county. Florida counties reporting locally acquired dengue in 2026. Hillsborough, Pinellas and Pasco, shaded dark, are under local states of emergency. Hillsborough alone accounts for 223 of the state's 252 cases. Counts are from the Florida Department of Health through September 26. Broward's first case was announced October 5. Pasco County declared a local emergency on September 23. Hillsborough and Pinellas followed the next day, and all three have extended their orders to October 8. The declarations rest on Chapter 252 of the Florida Statutes , the same emergency-management law counties invoke before a hurricane. What the orders unlock shows what slows mosquito control in ordinary times. Pinellas's resolution suspends the usual rules for contracts, purchasing and hiring. It authorizes crews "to enter upon lands during daylight hours, whether public or private," and to remove "without notice, stagnant water or other breeding places for mosquitoes." Hillsborough's order, signed by County Administrator Bonnie Wise, also provides "a path for federal reimbursement of certain expenses," even as President Donald Trump has declared war on Washington, D.C.'s mosquitoes , ordering a 90% cut by 2028 in the capital's invasive mosquitoes, including the same species now spreading dengue in Tampa. The right of entry is the provision to watch. Aedes aegypti , the mosquito behind this outbreak, breeds in flowerpot saucers, buckets, tires and bottle caps, almost always within a few yards of a house. The decisive habitat is private property. In normal times a crew that finds a neglected backyard full of standing water has to knock, wait and come back. During a dengue outbreak, that backyard stops being a public nuisance and becomes a public hazard. Hillsborough's mosquito program had never faced dengue on this scale. For assistance, the county signed mutual-aid agreements with Manatee and Pasco counties for helicopters and pilots, and cross-trained Public Works employees to canvass neighborhoods and run nighttime truck spraying. Lee County's mosquito district flew in four helicopters , which spray larvicide by day and adulticide by night. Insecticide alone has cost the county about $250,000 so far. Spraying from the air is a blunt tool against this particular mosquito. Ae. aegypti bites during the day and rests indoors and in sheltered corners, where spray from a helicopter or a truck has little reach. Miami-Dade and Lee counties have spent the decade since Zika building programs aimed specifically at it, including releases of male mosquitoes infected with Wolbachia , a bacterium that causes their matings with wild females to produce eggs that never hatch. Miami-Dade receives more than ten times as many travelers with dengue as Hillsborough does, and so far it has eight local cases this year. More infected travelers would be the obvious explanation, but the total barely moved. Through late September, Florida had 245 travel-associated cases, up about 11% from 220 at the same point in 2025 . Hillsborough's went from 11 to 13. Tampa's summer, per the National Weather Service , was less than a degree above normal and drier than usual. What changed was where imported infections came from and which serotype they carried. Of this year's 245 travel-associated cases, 201 were in people infected during recent travel to Cuba , up from 140 a year earlier. Last year most such infections were DENV-4, one of dengue's four serotypes. This year most are DENV-2, as Cuba's epidemic runs far above normal and fuel shortages curtail fumigation on the island. Imported DENV-2 cases rose roughly eightfold, from 22 to 183. Of the 232 local Florida cases that have been typed, 230 are DENV-2. Tampa's outbreak began with a virus carried in from Cuba, where fuel shortages are curtailing mosquito control. To a mosquito-borne virus, the two places form one transmission system, joined by daily flights. Each infection prevented in the Caribbean is one fewer spark landing in Florida, which gives the United States a direct stake in controlling dengue beyond its own borders. The best evidence on how Florida's outbreaks start comes from a preprint posted in May by Nathan Grubaugh's group at the Yale School of Public Health and the Florida Department of Health. They sequenced dengue from 133 local and 294 travel cases between 2010 and 2024. Florida's outbreaks, they found, were caused by "frequent short-lived DENV introductions, primarily from the Caribbean," and they found no evidence that any lineage survived from one season to the next. The paper has not yet been peer reviewed. Tampa will test that history. A single serotype across nearly every typed case sounds more like one introduction that ran away, although it also fits several separate introductions of the same serotype. Dengue expert and Northeastern University Professor Michael Johansson has said it is unclear whether the outbreak is one sustained chain or "multiple smaller introductions." One chain would mean Tampa kept the virus's reproduction number above one for months. The Associated Press calls it the largest outbreak in the continental United States in decades. Hawaii's Big Island had 264 local cases between September 2015 and March 2016, a dozen more than Florida's current count. Florida's next weekly report will probably pass it. Hawaii's outbreak lasted six months. Florida's has run about three so far. The Asian tiger mosquito , which spreads its bites among animals as well as people, was the main culprit during the Hawaii outbreak. Tampa's is fueled by Ae. aegypti , which feeds almost exclusively on humans, in a metro area of three million people. None of this approaches Puerto Rico, the US territory where dengue is endemic and case counts run much higher than in any of the 50 states . Florida is not the only state seeing local spread. Virginia confirmed its first locally acquired dengue case in August. Following publication of an outbreak investigation of a cluster of dengue cases in Los Angeles, I argued that rising introductions will continue to push marginal places over the transmission threshold. Tampa appears to have crossed that boundary this year by a substantial margin. The emergency orders expire Thursday unless renewed. Cooler nights in November will probably do more to end this outbreak than spraying from helicopters. The Yale team's genomes, covering 2010 through 2024, show no lineage surviving the winter. The last documented case of that was in Key West in 2009 and 2010 . If Tampa's DENV-2 comes back next summer before any new strain is introduced, it will be the first since. 儿童大脑中的登革热:巴基斯坦放射学家绘制病毒的成像指纹 - Bioengineer.org

Dengue in Children’s Brains: Pakistani Radiologists Map the Virus’s Imaging Fingerprints - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报巴基斯坦卡拉奇阿迦汗大学医院的放射科医生团队对儿科登革热的影像学特征进行了系统的图片回顾,发表在《儿科放射学》上。该研究将这些发现分为三组:直接病毒入侵、免疫介导的损伤和可逆性水肿综合征。这种分类对于指导临床管理至关重要,因为每个类别都有不同的预后和 治疗 含义。研究强调,登革热可导致严重的神经系统并发症,包括脑炎、脊髓炎症和儿童瘫痪。它还可以识别特定的成像模式,例如双甜甜圈征和杰克灯笼征,它们分别与 重症登革热 脑炎和菱形脑炎相关。这些迹象对于早期 诊断 和干预非常重要。该研究强调放射科医生需要认识到这些模式以提供适当的护理,因为某些情况可能通过 治疗 的支持性逆转。巴基斯坦经历了多次登革热暴发,其中包括2022年的一次重大流行,这凸显了这项研究在登革热流行地区的相关性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于 2026 年 10 月 6 日,该事件指的是绘制儿童大脑中的登革热成像指纹图谱,这是了解该病毒影响的最新进展。
地点与对象
该研究在巴基斯坦卡拉奇进行,重点关注受登革热影响并出现神经系统症状的儿童。
事件
巴基斯坦卡拉奇阿迦汗大学医院的放射科医生团队对儿科登革热的影像学特征进行了系统的图片回顾,并根据病理生理学对其进行了分类。《儿科放射学》的出版物证实了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
该综述强调了儿童登革热潜在的神经并发症,包括脑炎、脊髓炎症和瘫痪。它还指出了早期识别对于指导适当管理的重要性,其中一些情况可以通过支持性护理来逆转。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究提供了一个通过 MRI 诊断登革热相关神经系统疾病的框架,强调了临床背景、血清学检测和了解特定病变模式的必要性。这是经过确认的诊断审查,没有任何未解决信息的迹象。
